## Claude

The pit work for FlowWorks is built: `adba8a19d1`, `8100177f21` and `bac9174bbf`. Its tests pass (318/318 and 4/4). Nothing is deployed yet, and the in-game checks for it are on the list live round 2 works through.

- **Pits now trap by height.** Pathfinding refuses any step too tall for the creature, and it sees dry pond beds as hollows too. The old pit-avoidance rules are only used if you switch this off. There's a new "Slow climb factor" setting, default 2.
  - **This changes colonists, not just animals.** Per the design, colonists obey the same climb rule. A ladder only gets anyone out if it stands against a wall.
- **Ramps and ladders.** There's a new pit ramp: one square, faces uphill, climbs up to 3 levels, no slowdown. Ladders now work at any depth but slowly. New settings: Ramps, Levels a ramp spans, and Ladder slowness. The ramp uses a marked stand-in (a vanilla arrow) until you approve real art.
- **Digging in a dry pond bed** now goes deeper from the bed: a depth-2 rim digs to 3. A depth-4 middle can't go to 5 yet, because the game currently stops at depth 4. Those digs are refused until the existing deeper-digging item (droids taking over the deep digging) lands.

Two new helpers started:
- **FlowWorks code:** corrects two comments that point to files that don't exist. It makes the flood switch's setting text tell the truth; with that switch off, a spill currently produces nothing. Then it reviews every FlowWorks code file and checks the Mod Settings for gaps, toward getting FlowWorks ready to ship. It is the only helper editing FlowWorks code.
- **River test map:** finds out why no pawn is ever swept away and the gold pan never finds anything on that map. Two in-game checks are stuck behind it: river weirs and quarry digging. It works offline only and leaves the game to live round 2.

I'm holding two bigger builds until you want them: deep lakes (ponds and lakes deeper than 4 levels) and the Struck Glass lightning rite for Pyrelands.

- **panning_draws_stock:** the C# probe pans once, at a 5–11% hit chance. That one pan comes after five sluice washes have drained the stock (11.2 → 0.2), so even a hit would come back as "stock spent".
- **flood_refill:** it is UNMEASURED by design (marked owed in the harness), not because of the site.

**Fix:** published `d69c958712`, ledger `1acc103f601b`. `returns_home` now places its own throwaway colonists in the river at the map edge. Panning now retries with the pan yield and river stock raised, puts both settings back afterwards, and checks that the stock actually went down by what was panned. Selftests 2/2. I also added the missing `pitRampsEnabled` entry in `validation.py` to the owed list, because without it the extensions selftest failed. That gap came from the pit-ramps commit.

**Live check R1 (~8 min, in `Transient/experiment_2026-10-14/live_worklist.md`):** run `river_site.py`, then `extension_proof.py --live --site river --chains swept,placer`. Pass bar: both rows PASS and the two settings read back 1 and 2.

## Claude

The river test map wasn't the problem: the test script couldn't take a reading. That's fixed in the Python (`d69c958712`), and the in-game check is still owed.

- **"Nobody swept away":** the test only read the swept-away list. The map keeps its colonists drafted on dry ground, and nothing ever put a pawn in the river, so nobody could be swept. The mod's own sweep code reads correctly. The test now puts its own throwaway colonists into the current at the map edge.
- **"Pan found nothing":** the test panned once, with a 5–11% chance of a find. That one try came after the sluices had already drained the river's stock to almost nothing. It now raises the pan yield and the river stock, retries, checks the stock went down by what was panned, then puts both settings back.
- **Flood refill:** this row was always meant to be "can't measure yet". The test script lists it as owed work.

The new check takes about 8 minutes and is on round 2's list. It also unblocks the river-weir and quarry-digging checks. The helper also fixed a test failure the pit-ramps change had caused: the new ramps setting was missing from the test script's list of known settings.

**Mods.** The only change is in the test list's definition, not in what loads: it now declares `mandrake.rm.patches` (the texture fix for the Star Wars animals). Rounds 1 and 2 had added that mod by hand, so the test recorder refused to log those runs. The test list stays at 23 mods. Round 3 deploys all of them from one code version.

**Explosive Plant Growth: the planner recommends keeping it out, and I agree.** It reads FlowWorks correctly at the current code. But it speeds up all plant growth, so the Pyrelands growth tests could no longer tell whether Pyrelands caused what they see. Its own test script also has no irrigation check yet. It should get its own short test session.

**Monitoring in round 3:**
- **Before launch:** the companion is rebuilt with FOUNDRY's health tools included.
- **During the run:** health recording stays on for the whole session. Its readout of how much it slows the game is the proof FOUNDRY still owes. A 10-minute profile runs during the Pyrelands tests, alongside the vitals monitor and telemetry.

**A real bug it found, now filed and being fixed.** When about 30 small mods were merged into the combined biomes mod, any "load only if mod X is present" guard that still names one of the old mods became permanently false. Nothing logs it, so:
- FlowWorks' four liquid-works ruins never generate on any mod list;

<result>Art review sheets now refuse to show stale pictures, and the Pyrelands sheet from tonight is correctly reported stale (9 of 9 rows). All 10 touched selftests pass (10/10).

**The rule:** a sheet that is served must match what is installed now. Anything it labels "in game" must be the exact bytes of a file in our mods today (checked by content hash). Anything our mods have installed for a texture the sheet shows must appear on it as installed. Every texture our defs draw, read from the mod XML (`src/`) at that moment, must appear if we have art for it. The thumbnails must be the ones built. Disk and the XML are re-read every time; nothing is cached.

**How it's enforced:**
- **Build:** the sheet gate has a new requirement 15, so a sheet that is stale when built is never written, and `refresh_sheets.py` rebuilds one that goes stale later.
- **Serving:** `serve_gated.py` refuses to start a stale sheet. A running server re-checks on every page load and shows a refusal page if the sheet has gone stale.
- **Texture locations:** every row, creatures and plants alike, now takes its texture paths from the mod XML at build, not the Oct-7 census. A trial Leaning Scrub rebuild corrected 17 rows and passed.
- **Install:** `art.py install` and `enact --apply` now print which sheets they just made stale.

**Old sheets:** they are checked from the row data already embedded in them, so no rebuild was needed to check them. Of 39 sheets, 20 are fresh, 16 are stale and 3 can't be checked. Stale ones won't serve until rebuilt.

Commits: `850c6b0cdf` (code and design), `9e89b99783` (item and ledger), both pushed.

- Any old art sheet he opens now will be refused until it is rebuilt; the rebuild is the next step on the item.
- `--serve-stale-i-know-it-shows-old-art` serves a stale sheet anyway, with a red banner listing the stale rows. He may want that override removed.

## Claude

Yes, RimPulse needed changing, and it's done (`efc3efdb19f8`). Both alarms were false, for clear reasons:

- **"Game froze" alarms:** these were two of our own test-map setup calls holding the game for 10 to 11 seconds each. The calls export a world tile and generate a map, which takes that long. The freeze alarm now ignores pauses that a bridge call held for 5 seconds or more, while real freezes still fire.
- **"Exception storm" alarm:** this was about 30 errors arriving in a split second, which the alarm reported as 360 per second. It now needs at least 20 errors over at least a second.
- **The errors themselves:** the bridge's delete-everything-in-an-area tool tried to delete steam geysers and void monoliths, which the game won't allow. It now skips anything the game marks as not destroyable. That fix needs a companion redeploy, which is owed for the next round.

The alarm code is read from this window's copy of the code, so RimPulse should use the new rules from its next one-minute check. I haven't confirmed that yet.

**The OOM kill was a pile-up of art-sheet viewers.** The art-sheet helper had left 27 sheet servers running at about 190 MB each, roughly 5.1 GB. This window's command sandbox is capped at 6 GB, so Linux killed two Python processes at 20:37. I've stopped all 27 servers, and this window's memory use dropped from 7.9 GB to 4.5 GB. I've told that helper to stop starting a server per sheet and to make the rebuild tool clean up after itself. Round 3 runs its own test processes and didn't report a problem. The two killed processes were probably the helper's sheet rebuilds; I haven't confirmed whose they were.

## Claude

That alarm is false. It isn't errors at all, and the earlier fix didn't catch it.

**What it actually counted** (measured from the game's own per-second records): the "exception" count includes *suppressed repeats*. These are log calls of the "report this error only once" kind, which the game swallows after the first time. Round 3's map has something making about 6 of those calls per frame, roughly 360 per second. Over the same windows, real errors and warnings were **0**. RimPulse added the swallowed repeats in, so it read a quiet log as a storm. The earlier fix (at least 20 over at least a second) didn't help, because 360 a second passes that test easily.

<result>The bug was in the mod, not the row, and it's fixed and published at `c238935c98`. Selftests: 3/3 green, including the C# selftest at 322/322.

**Cause:** MEASURED by reading the code (no live run). The ladder never told the cliff masks it exists. The ladder is walkable with path cost 0, so the game sends the pathfinder no cell change when one is built. Only the climb generation tells the masks, and the ladder bumped it only on raise/lower. The row's "lower" on a ladder already lowered changes nothing. So A* kept refusing the lip↔pit step. That explains P4, P5n, and P4f's "not held, walked out False". The other two leads are ruled out: a 1×1 pit puts the ladder against a wall on every side, and the row's staging is fine.

**Fix:** a built or removed ladder now bumps the generation and clears the reachability cache, the same way ramps already do. A new selftest pins the D4 climb down and out.

## Claude

The ladder bug was in the mod, and it's fixed (`c238935c98`). All three tests for it pass, including FlowWorks' own suite at 322 of 322.

The cause was that a ladder never told the new climbing rules it was there. Ladders are walkable at no extra cost, so building one doesn't tell the game's pathfinder anything changed. The ladder only announced itself when it was raised or lowered. In the test, telling an already-lowered ladder to lower did nothing, so the pathfinder kept refusing the step down into the pit. Now building or removing a ladder updates the climbing rules, the same way ramps already did. A new test pins a colonist climbing down into and back out of a 4-deep pit.

**Round 4 has started.** It deploys everything from one new code version that includes tonight's fixes, then reruns the three pilot suites. It also runs the two in-game checks that are left: the anima-tree ban and the Pyrelands site temperature check. If criteria 1 to 5 are met, it closes the pilot and leaves your sign-off (criterion 6) as one line for you to answer. After that it works the check list:
- ladders, the liquid-works ruins and the other three mods' skipped patches;
- the pond-bed hollow's deep middle and walk-out;
- the reload fixes;
- the quiet test-site setup, recording where any uninvited monsters come from.

It will leave the tall-tank and Pyrelands art items for you. The GimmeSomeSlack slowdown investigation is still running offline. I've told it round 4 has the game, and it can add short probes for round 4 to run.

## Claude

All three slowdowns traced to our own code, and none was the mods running slower. All are fixed offline; none is proven in the game yet.

- **GimmeSomeSlack's flying section** went from 50 seconds to 137, and the cause was a bug in our test script. On a freshly launched game the safe tick-advance tool's first status reply has an empty field, the bridge drops empty fields, and the test script concluded the tool didn't exist. It fell back to an older stepping method, which made 50 calls of up to 10 seconds each. Round 2 got lucky: something earlier in that game had already used the tool. Fixed in `9a4852f50a`.
- **The 360 swallowed log calls per second:** six of our Utinni factions have no leader type set. The base game's faction update reports that "once" on every tick for each faction: 6 × 60 = 360 a second. They now have leader types (`cf73c55411`). Saves made before the fix still have the empty leaders, so older test saves will keep showing it.
- **Profiler gap:** the profiler now times 18 methods across all three mods, not just FlowWorks. The error counter also records *which* call is repeating, so a future case like this names itself. That's in the bridge companion and needs a deploy.

## Claude

I've recorded your rejection on the tall-tank item in your own words (`b07b1bb82932`), and a helper is working on it in three steps:

1. **Research.** It takes real tall art from the base game, the DLCs and any well-known installed mods: walls, ship parts, reactors, generators, deep drills and tall machines. From each it measures how much of the picture is the top versus the front, how flat a round top looks (which gives the viewing angle), whether vertical edges stay parallel, where the light comes from, and how height is shown above the footprint. It turns those measurements into one written rule for the camera angle, in `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\design\RimMandrake\building_camera_perspective_2026-10-14.md`, with a reference sheet of the examples it measured. It also checks our short tanks against that rule. If they break it too, it will tell you, but it won't change them without your OK.
2. **Redraw both tall tanks to that rule.** Same family look, same 2×2 base, a taller front face, and a round top drawn at the measured angle.
3. **Nothing goes into the game until you've seen it.** You'll get a review sheet and a picture, `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\Transient\tall_tanks_2026-10-14b\tall_tanks_v2.png`, showing the current art next to the new, beside walls and a vanilla tall machine at game scale.

Once it has measured the camera angle, it will add the rule to the sprite-generation skill (`generating-rimworld-sprites`). The skill itself gets a short rule plus a pointer, and the full measurements go in a reference file beside it, linked to the design doc.

Normally skills are only edited in dedicated cleanup sessions, so the commit will cite your request as the authority. Its report will list every skill file it changed.

<result>I reviewed both files in full and found two real defects in the main settings file, both now fixed. Both files are marked CLEAN, everything is pushed, and the targeted selftests pass 4/4.

**Fixed:**
- **F1 — "Reset all" skipped the follow-up refreshes.** Some settings need the game to redo something when they change: recompute where creatures can climb out of pits, rebuild pit rooms, redraw cut walls, or switch the liquid looks on or off. Reset-all runs inside its confirmation dialog, after the settings window has finished drawing, and the old code only noticed changes made within the window's own drawing. So a confirmed Reset-all changed those settings without redoing any of that. Now one check compares each setting with its last-applied value before and after every frame, so every way of changing a setting is caught. That includes the rule that forces a feature back off when its game hook failed to load, and changes made through the bridge.
- **F2 — the pond budget slider was greyed out wrongly.** `sourceBudgetMultiplier` is read whenever a pond is first found, even with "Sources have a stock" off, and that pond keeps the resulting size for good. So it was not inert with its master switch off. I made it independent, so the slider stays usable.
- **Hose tidy-ups:** the hose test hook's reset had its own hand-kept copy of every default, so it now calls the same reset as the Reset-all button. I also corrected a code comment that wasn't true.

**Checked, nothing wrong:**
- Every other master→dependent pair is truly inert with its master off. I read the code at every place each setting is used. One borderline case: the scorch fade time still ages old scorch marks while scorch is off, but nothing is drawn, so I left it greyed.
- Saved settings round-trip: all 112 fields are saved under their own name with matching defaults, and no names clash with the other settings sections.
- No per-game state is stored in statics in either file.

I also updated two existing C# source checks that asserted the old approach. One gap: I didn't break the code on purpose to prove those updated checks can actually fail. One live check is still owed (no bridge this session): turn "Pits trap by relative height" off, confirm Reset-all, then read the pit climb state in game to see it was recomputed.

<result>I found the likely cause of the ladder failures (P4, P4f, P5n) and fixed it, but only offline: no deploy and no live run yet, so whether those rows now pass is still inferred.

1. **Cause (from the game's decompiled 1.6 source).** Before every walk, the game checks whether the destination can be reached, and that check passes no pawn. Our pit rule (`RelativeVeto`) gives a query with no pawn no ladder. So it refused every walk down into a pit with a lowered ladder and every walk back out, for colonists too, and the walk order was cancelled before the pawn took a step.
2. **Why it explains all three rows (inferred).** In P4f the trapped pawn can't climb out. That pawn then stands on the only pit cell, so P4's colonist can't get in. P5n is the same refusal on an empty pit. P1, P3 and the climb proof never walk a ladder, which is why they stayed green.
3. **What I ruled out by reading.** The cliff masks and the per-pawn mask swap, the ladder-closing grid (it does nothing for colonists), the per-step climb check, the step cost and the pit-room region split.
4. **Fix (`b98094a832`).** A query with no pawn now counts any lowered ladder, the same as the cliff masks do. A query for a specific pawn is unchanged: hostiles are still kept off ladders, and raised ladders still block everyone. The decision now lives in two small functions in `RM_PocketGridMath.cs` (`LadderIsAWay`, `ReachVetoed`), which `RM_PocketGrid.UsableLadderIn` and `RelativeVeto` call.
5. **Offline reproduction.** A new case in the FlowWorks C# self-test, `Pawnless_reach_counts_a_lowered_ladder_as_the_masks_do`, shows the old rule refusing both directions and the new rule allowing them. The C# self-test passes 324/324 and the targeted Python suite run passes 1/1. The FlowWorks build succeeded, and the DLL and `.srchash` are committed.
6. **Next live check.** Added to `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\Transient\experiment_2026-10-14\live_worklist.md`: deploy FlowWorks at `b98094a832` and run `validation_v2 --live --fresh-map` phase P. ## Claude

The ladder bug is likely found and fixed (`b98094a832`), but only offline so far. Whether the three ladder rows now pass in game is still unproven.

**The cause, read from the game's own code:** before any walk, the game checks whether the destination can be reached at all. It runs that check without a specific pawn, and our pit rule gave a "no pawn" check no ladder. So every walk down into a pit with a lowered ladder, and every walk back out, was refused before the pawn took a step. That was true for colonists too. The other rows never walk a ladder, which is why they stayed green.

**The fix:** a check with no pawn now counts any lowered ladder, the same way the climbing rules already do. Checks for a specific pawn are unchanged, so hostiles are still kept off ladders and a raised ladder still blocks everyone. A new test reproduces the old refusal offline and shows the fix, and FlowWorks' own tests pass 324/324. If round 5 still shows a red row, the next step is to read the pawn's current job before trying a fourth fix.

The same helper fully reviewed its 5 FlowWorks code files and found no defects. A reviewer is now reading the 3 files that tonight's fixes changed, in full: the two pit files and the overhead-wires test.

Round 5 starts once that and the Pyrelands review are done. Round 5 will also fix the test list's mismatch over the Star Wars Animal Collection.

<result>**Verdict:** I found a real defect in `modset_builder.py`, fixed it, re-reviewed the whole file, and it is now marked CLEAN again.

**The defect:** commit c4cbb5c66c added the "is the live list still the tier list?" check only when taking a new before-copy, not to `--restore` itself. `--restore` still copied the before-copy over whatever was live. So in the case where the owner put a newer list back by hand and the swap marker stayed behind, `--restore` wrote the older pre-swap list over his newer one, said "verified" and deleted the marker. That is the "restores yesterday" bug the item was meant to close. I reproduced it on the pre-fix code using a scratch copy.

**The fix:** `--restore` now writes only when the live file is exactly the tier list that swap wrote. If the live file already matches the before-copy, it just clears the old marker and writes nothing. Anything else is refused, and the refusal message names the manual way out. I also fixed a minor case: a malformed marker used to crash `--restore` (nothing was written); it is now refused cleanly.

**Selftest:** passes (temp folders only; the live ModsConfig.xml was never touched). Its three new cases fail on the pre-fix code. `--restore-full` and `--tier --apply` had no defect: they can't write anything but the owner's list (except `--restore-full`'s full list, which is by design), and they can't destroy the active before-copy.

**Commits:** ff00e81890 (fix), 5db083c7b6 (clean record), published. Review note: `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\Transient\experiment_2026-10-14\review_modset.md` (it appears in that mirror after `./mirror sync`; in the bench clone now).</result>

- **Why A2 couldn't be measured:** a real "no room to place" can't be set up on a map. When the breach moves a stack of held catch, its old cell is left empty and sits inside the engine's ~13-cell placement search, so the stack always fits back there. The existing breach row only checks the normal case.
- **What changed:** a static `washPlaceValidator` on the weir (null in normal play, so nothing changes for players) is handed to the breach's item placement. A new probe, `RM_RiverWorksProofWorks.ProofBreachNoRoom`, sets it to refuse every cell for one breach, so the engine's own placement search fails exactly as it would with no room. The probe then reports whether every held stack is still on the map, at its old cell, with the same number of units. If no placement was ever attempted it returns UNMEASURED, not PASS. The new script row is `works/breach_wash_no_room_keeps_catch` in `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\src\RimMandrake\FlowWorks\northstar\extensions_rivers.py`. The hook is in the JawaBench companion — that wasn't touched.
- **Checks:** the build had 0 warnings and 0 errors, and the 3 targeted selftests pass (FlowWorks stock, rivers, extensions). All three changed files were fully reviewed with nothing found and marked clean at `cc964dd4a4`.
- **Live command that proves A2** (needs the bridge, the FlowWorks DLL deployed and a `river_site.py` map): `python.exe src/RimMandrake/FlowWorks/northstar/extension_proof.py --live --site river --chains works | tr -d '\r'`. A single call also works: `jawa/static_call type=RimMandrake.FlowWorks.Rivers.RM_RiverWorksProofWorks method=ProofBreachNoRoom args=-`. It passes when `asked&gt;0`, `spawned = atOld = held &gt; 0`, `unitsAfter = unitsBefore` and `breaching=True`.
- **What is left:** deploy, run the live row, then verify A2. The exact command is in the item's ledger note. One extra: `src/RimMandrake/Utils/modcheck/required_checks.json` (the modcheck manifest) was already out of date before this change. I did not commit a rebuild of it, because it would have captured another builder's uncommitted `Pyrelands/validation.py`, so the manifest doesn't list the new row until someone rebuilds it.

## Claude

I've built a way to measure the weir check but haven't run it in the game yet (code in `cc964dd4a4`, notes in `27b89dcdac`). The build had no warnings or errors, the three related self-tests pass, and all three changed files were fully reviewed and marked clean.

The check never had a measurement because the "no room to put the catch back" case couldn't be set up on a real map. A moved stack always fits back into the cell it just left. The new hook makes the game's own placement search refuse every cell for a single breach. Then it checks that every stack of held catch stays where it was, with the same count. In normal play the hook is off, so nothing changes for players. If the breach never tries to place anything, the check reports "not measured" rather than a pass.

The new test row is in `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\src\RimMandrake\FlowWorks\northstar\extensions_rivers.py`. The mod checker's list of required checks was already out of date before this, so the new row won't appear there until someone rebuilds it. <note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>The test's baseline was wrong, not the reader. I fixed the test, it passes 9/9, and the item is closed against `21ccec33e5`.

- **Cause:** the baseline sorted events by `(ts, file rank)`. When two events in different shards share a second, that puts them in shard-name order (BENCH before OWNER), not the order they were written. So BENCH's lease came before OWNER's reassign of `PIT_POCKET_GRID_BUILD_SLOPES_NEVER_TRAP`, and the baseline logged a lease refusal that never happened. The reader orders by the `tsn` stamp and was right, so I left it unchanged.
- **Fix:** the baseline is now the order events were actually appended: a stable sort on `(ts, tsn, file rank)` that keeps file position. It doesn't call the reader's sort or tie-break rules, so it is still an independent check. Events from before `tsn` existed sort exactly as they did under the old baseline. I also added `t_real_ledger_survives_union_scramble`: it shuffles a scratch copy of the real ledger, duplicates lines and moves some between shards, then requires the same result as the real ledger.
- **Proof it still catches a real divergence:** I ran the test against broken copies of `model.py` in the scratchpad; every ledger file's md5 was unchanged before and after.
  - A reader that orders same-second events by seat (the old baseline's flaw, built into the reader) fails only the real-ledger baseline case.
  - Turning off duplicate removal fails 3 cases, and keeping file order fails 5; both real-ledger cases fail in each.
  - Dropping `tsn` from the sort is caught only by the small synthetic `tsn` case, because the live ledger has no event pair where `tsn` alone changes the result.
- **Results:** `selftest_reader_invariant.py` 9/9. `run_selftests --only` on it plus `selftest_model.py`, `selftest_concurrency.py` and `selftest_built_old_reader.py`: 4/4, GREEN.
- **Commits:** code `21ccec33e5`, ledger and review record `6bf85024a5`, both pushed with `./publish`.
- **CLEAN:** I reviewed the whole file and fixed one docstring error (it said "1.0 ms"; the gap is 1.05 s). Nothing else came up, and it is marked CLEAN at `21ccec33e5`.

Write-up: `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\Transient\experiment_2026-10-14\reader_invariant_fix.md`</result>

**GPT consults.** Each consult gets a pack about its mod:
- the design goals and your rulings;
- known behaviours and past bugs, from the items, tonight's round write-ups and the lessons;
- how we debug RimWorld, from the debug process doc, the engine facts and the instrument traps;
- the mod's full source, definitions and settings.

**What GPT is asked to hunt for:** hidden problems such as save/reload state, data keyed by ID that carries over between games, multiple maps, conflicts with other mods, cost per tick, settings changed mid-game, and places where the code doesn't match the design. FlowWorks is too large for one consult, so it is split by theme; each part still gets the full design and debugging background.

**GPT's answers are leads, not facts.** Each agent checks every finding against the code and sorts it:
- real: it read the code and confirmed it;
- needs a live check: it writes down the exact check to run;
- rejected: it says why.

It files an item for every real or needs-a-live-check finding and writes a `TRIAGE.md` per mod in `...\release_push_2026-10-15\gpt_<Mod>\`. Those items will feed the live rounds and the walkthrough cards.

<result>I ran 11 GPT consults over FlowWorks and checked every finding against the code. GPT returned 110 findings: 56 are confirmed in the code, 25 are plausible but need the check written in their row, and 29 I rejected. I didn't change any code. The confirmed and plausible ones are filed as 33 items for BENCH, grouped where findings share a fix.

The FlowWorks code was split into 9 themed slices. Two slices took longer than the 10-minute limit and were each split in two. One of those halves (the hose geometry maths and settings import) still timed out, so it ran at medium effort and without the known-bugs list.

Top 5 by severity:
1. **Machine turn-taking** (`LIQUID_ROTATION_FIX_STARVED_STRANGER`, HIGH, confirmed): it is tracked per liquid across the whole map. A short-supplied machine on another network can stall a well-supplied one forever. Also, a machine that takes several liquids never rotates between them.
2. **Pond capacity can multiply** (`POND_CAPACITY_FIX_DOUBLING_BED`, HIGH, confirmed): digging out a pond bed's squares and filling them back in another order raises its capacity. With bed depths 2 and 4, capacity goes from 6 to 12.
3. **Bottle jobs bypass the pond's stock** (`BOTTLE_STOCK_MEND_FREE_REFILLS`, HIGH, confirmed): filling from pond terrain never debits the pond, and washing uses no water. Pouring those bottles into a tank creates liquid.
4. **Ferry rope not built until something reads it** (`FERRY_ROPE_FIX_BUILT_ONLY_WHEN_ASKED`, HIGH, confirmed): after pairing or a load, the rope stays unbuilt until something enters the river, so undrafted pawns' routes ignore the ferry. Today's round-9 ferry test reads the rope count first, which hides this.
5. **Pit reachability for flyers and big pawns** (`PIT_REACH_VERIFY_FLYERS_AND_GIANTS`, HIGH, plausible): the check vanilla runs before every walk ignores flight and uses the person climb limit. This is the same kind of bug as the ladder fix, and could refuse valid routes for flyers and large climbers. Next is `RAMP_MASKS_VERIFY_STALE_SLOPE` (HIGH, plausible): building or removing a ramp may leave pathing stale, the bug the ladder had.

GPT got three things wrong: unbalanced braces in two river files (they balance), multi-cell pit covers (all three ship 1×1), and tar glass forming inside channels (excavated cells are skipped).

Everything is in `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\Transient\release_push_2026-10-15\gpt_FlowWorks\`; the triage table is `TRIAGE.md` there. Commit 4c4371e21c, published.</result>

All pushed (last publish `fe3ab44cbe`). Not deployed, so the game doesn't have the fixes yet.

**Defects fixed:**
- **D1 (`RM_LiquidFire.cs`):** turning "Astrofuel detonates" off mid-chain left the remaining Bomb blasts firing. The schedule is now dropped, the same as when fire is turned off.
- **D2 (`RM_LiquidFire.cs`):** the static list used for the throw kept pawn references after each use, including into the next game. It is now cleared.

- **D4 (`EmpirePursuit.csproj`):** the header comment gave the wrong assembly name and said to build from the read-only mirror. Corrected.
- **D5 (`extensions.py`):** the `fuel_moat_astrofuel` proof would FAIL if rerun on the same map within a day, because of the one-raid-per-day limit. It now reports UNMEASURED instead.

- **Freezes:** every per-tick limit holds, and a blast lighting chemfuel never re-enters the loop mid-run.
- **Chemfuel:** the fire always finishes and the fuel can't go below zero.

## Claude

The fuel-moat review found and fixed five real bugs (`fe3ab44cbe`). These fixes haven't been deployed or tried in the game yet.

- **Switching astrofuel off mid-blast** left the remaining blasts going off. They now stop.
- **Colonists thrown by a blast** stayed referenced in memory, even into the next game. That's now cleared.
- **The "investigating raid"** could have been sent to a temporary map the player had already left. Raids now come only to home maps; elsewhere only visibility rises.
- **The astrofuel proof** would fail if rerun on the same map within a day, because of the one-raid-a-day limit. It now reports "not measured" instead.
- **A project file comment** gave the wrong name and pointed builds at the read-only mirror. Corrected.

It found no way for a blast to freeze the game, and fires always burn out cleanly. Saving mid-blast or mid-fire is handled.

Ten large files were only partly read, so a last reviewer is reading them in full. It will also sweep all three mods, plus the Empire Pursuit code the raid hook uses, for anything else not yet reviewed clean. When it finishes I'll commit, push and write the handoff.
